User\n\n````\n<feedback>To run a Model Context Protocol (MCP) server as a systemd service using [Numtide's system-manager](https://github.com/numtide/system-manager), you need to expose your server over HTTP or a Unix domain socket (SSE). Because systemd runs in the background, standard standard input/output (stdio) will not work directly. [1, 2, 3, 4] \nBelow is a complete declarative Nix Flake setup implementing a systemd service via system-manager. [1, 5] \n## 1. File Structure\nCreate a dedicated directory with the following structure:\n\nmy-mcp-systemd/\n├── flake.nix\n└── modules/\n    └── system.nix\n\n## 2. Configure the System Module\nDefine the systemd service syntax inside modules/system.nix. This example uses the popular python-based fastmcp runner or a standard node package. Adjust the ExecStart path, command arguments, and environment variables to fit your specific server. [2, 6] \n\n# modules/system.nix\n{ pkgs, ... }:\n\n{\n  # Specify packages required by your service environment\n  environment.systemPackages = [\n    pkgs.nodejs     # or pkgs.python3, depending on your MCP server\n  ];\n\n  # Define the systemd service\n  systemd.services.mcp-server = {\n    enable = true;\n    description = \"Model Context Protocol Server via system-manager\";\n    \n    # Ensure it starts after network infrastructure is ready\n    after = [ \"network.target\" ];\n    wantedBy = [ \"multi-user.target\" ];\n\n    serviceConfig = {\n      Type = \"simple\";\n      \n      # For system-manager, services run as root by default. \n      # It is best practice to scope it to a less privileged user.\n      User = \"nobody\"; \n      \n      # IMPORTANT: Use SSE/HTTP or Unix Socket. Stdio will fail in systemd background processes.\n      # Example utilizing a python-based fastmcp script configured for HTTP:\n      ExecStart = \"${pkgs.python3Packages.fastmcp}/bin/fastmcp run /path/to/your/server.py --transport http --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8000\";\n      \n      # Optional: Alternative Node.js based command string\n      # ExecStart = \"${pkgs.nodejs}/bin/node /path/to/mcp-server/index.js --port 8000\";\n\n      Restart = \"always\";\n      RestartSec = \"5s\";\n      \n      # Clean environment parameters\n      Environment = [\n        \"NODE_ENV=production\"\n        \"LOG_LEVEL=info\"\n      ];\n    };\n  };\n}\n\n## 3. Setup the Flake\nYour main flake.nix orchestrates building this configuration through the system-manager library. [5, 7] \n\n# flake.nix\n{\n  description = \"System Manager setup for running an MCP Server\";\n\n  inputs = {\n    nixpkgs.url = \"github:NixOS/nixpkgs/nixos-unstable\";\n    system-manager = {\n      url = \"github:numtide/system-manager\";\n      inputs.nixpkgs.follows = \"nixpkgs\";\n    };\n  };\n\n  outputs = { self, nixpkgs, system-manager, ... }: {\n    systemConfigs.default = system-manager.lib.makeSystemConfig {\n      modules = [\n        ./modules/system.nix\n      ];\n    };\n  };\n}\n\n## 4. Deploy the Configuration\nTo compile your custom system profile and safely activate the systemd service, execute the following commands inside your configuration folder: [8, 9] \n\n# Initialize system-manager tracking on the host system (only required once)\nnix run 'github:numtide/system-manager' -- init\n# Compile and switch live to your new systemd service profile\nnix run 'github:numtide/system-manager' -- switch --flake .\n\n## 5. Validate the Status\nOnce initialized, check that systemd successfully spawned your background process:\n\nsudo systemctl status mcp-server.service\n\n## 6. Client Connection\nConfigure your local AI workspace (like Claude Desktop) to query the background systemd service over Server-Sent Events (SSE) or HTTP instead of a command hook: [3, 4] \n\n{\n  \"mcpServers\": {\n    \"my-systemd-mcp\": {\n      \"url\": \"http://127.0.0\"\n    }\n  }\n}\n\nIf you need help tailoring this, tell me:\n\n* What programming language @[/] runtime (Python, Node, Go) your MCP server uses.\n* If it requires access to local host files or CLI binaries (which may impact systemd sandbox permissions). User\n\n````\n<feedback>To run a Model Context Protocol (MCP) server as a systemd service using [Numtide's system-manager](https://github.com/numtide/system-manager), you need to expose your server over HTTP or a Unix domain socket (SSE). Because systemd runs in the background, standard standard input/output (stdio) will not work directly. [1, 2, 3, 4] \nBelow is a complete declarative Nix Flake setup implementing a systemd service via system-manager. [1, 5] \n## 1. File Structure\nCreate a dedicated directory with the following structure:\n\nmy-mcp-systemd/\n├── flake.nix\n└── modules/\n    └── system.nix\n\n## 2. Configure the System Module\nDefine the systemd service syntax inside modules/system.nix. This example uses the popular python-based fastmcp runner or a standard node package. Adjust the ExecStart path, command arguments, and environment variables to fit your specific server. [2, 6] \n\n# modules/system.nix\n{ pkgs, ... }:\n\n{\n  # Specify packages required by your service environment\n  environment.systemPackages = [\n    pkgs.nodejs     # or pkgs.python3, depending on your MCP server\n  ];\n\n  # Define the systemd service\n  systemd.services.mcp-server = {\n    enable = true;\n    description = \"Model Context Protocol Server via system-manager\";\n    \n    # Ensure it starts after network infrastructure is ready\n    after = [ \"network.target\" ];\n    wantedBy = [ \"multi-user.target\" ];\n\n    serviceConfig = {\n      Type = \"simple\";\n      \n      # For system-manager, services run as root by default. \n      # It is best practice to scope it to a less privileged user.\n      User = \"nobody\"; \n      \n      # IMPORTANT: Use SSE/HTTP or Unix Socket. Stdio will fail in systemd background processes.\n      # Example utilizing a python-based fastmcp script configured for HTTP:\n      ExecStart = \"${pkgs.python3Packages.fastmcp}/bin/fastmcp run /path/to/your/server.py --transport http --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8000\";\n      \n      # Optional: Alternative Node.js based command string\n      # ExecStart = \"${pkgs.nodejs}/bin/node /path/to/mcp-server/index.js --port 8000\";\n\n      Restart = \"always\";\n      RestartSec = \"5s\";\n      \n      # Clean environment parameters\n      Environment = [\n        \"NODE_ENV=production\"\n        \"LOG_LEVEL=info\"\n      ];\n    };\n  };\n}\n\n## 3. Setup the Flake\nYour main flake.nix orchestrates building this configuration through the system-manager library. [5, 7] \n\n# flake.nix\n{\n  description = \"System Manager setup for running an MCP Server\";\n\n  inputs = {\n    nixpkgs.url = \"github:NixOS/nixpkgs/nixos-unstable\";\n    system-manager = {\n      url = \"github:numtide/system-manager\";\n      inputs.nixpkgs.follows = \"nixpkgs\";\n    };\n  };\n\n  outputs = { self, nixpkgs, system-manager, ... }: {\n    systemConfigs.default = system-manager.lib.makeSystemConfig {\n      modules = [\n        ./modules/system.nix\n      ];\n    };\n  };\n}\n\n## 4. Deploy the Configuration\nTo compile your custom system profile and safely activate the systemd service, execute the following commands inside your configuration folder: [8, 9] \n\n# Initialize system-manager tracking on the host system (only required once)\nnix run 'github:numtide/system-manager' -- init\n# Compile and switch live to your new systemd service profile\nnix run 'github:numtide/system-manager' -- switch --flake .\n\n## 5. Validate the Status\nOnce initialized, check that systemd successfully spawned your background process:\n\nsudo systemctl status mcp-server.service\n\n## 6. Client Connection\nConfigure your local AI workspace (like Claude Desktop) to query the background systemd service over Server-Sent Events (SSE) or HTTP instead of a command hook: [3, 4] \n\n{\n  \"mcpServers\": {\n    \"my-systemd-mcp\": {\n      \"url\": \"http://127.0.0\"\n    }\n  }\n}\n\nIf you need help tailoring this, tell me:\n\n* What programming language @[/] runtime (Python, Node, Go) your MCP server uses.\n* If it requires access to local host files or CLI binaries (which may impact systemd sandbox permissions). User\n\n````\n<feedback>DASL is dasl.ing our project. DASL (\"dazzle\") is a small set of simple, standard primitives for working with content-addressed, linked data. It builds on content addressing, a proven approach used in Git and IPFS to create reliable content identifiers (known as CIDs) through cryptographic hashing. Content addressing enables robust data integrity checks and efficient networking: systems can verify they received exactly what they asked for and avoid downloading the same content twice. The linked data part lets you link to stuff by its hash. You can build very big graphs with these primitives, such as the graph behind Bluesky.\n\nWe call DASL \"data-addressed\" because it supports a data serialization component that makes content-addressing sweet and easy when working with data. The design is inspired by subcomponents of the IPFS universe, but simplified to improve interoperability, decrease costs, and work well with the web. More specifically, our priorities are:\n\npave the cowpaths: we focus on supporting what people trying to solve real-world problems actually use. This takes over any consideration of engineering ideals or theoretical purity. We're retconning the spec to what people actually use implement — as it should be.\nextensibility vs optionality: extensibility is important for long-lived distributed systems, because the world will happen and you will need to change. But introducing optionality reduces interoperability and increases cost of both implementation and adoption. So rather than require support for many options now, we have extension points now but deliberately don't use their full range.\ndon't make me think: you don't want to be thinking about content addressing. You want to grab this off the shelf and have something that works out of the box. Nothing weird, no impedance mismatch with the systems you know and love (or maybe know and hate, but whatever, it just works).\nlightweight loading: some people like JavaScript, others don't. We don't care, we just want things that work. What's certain is that you can't ignore it and be relevant. The ability to ship small code to the browser is critical.\nUnix philosophy: all of our specs are tiny and meant to compose together in simple ways that can be implemented independently from one another.\nThis is intended to work for the community, to grow support for what we need.
